## GHSA-4p3g-4hcj-wpvx · github.com/prebid/prebid-server

2026-07-29 · Go · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application was vulnerable to Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) because it constructed outbound HTTP requests using user-controlled input (e.g., 'endpoint', 'host', 'account') without sufficient validation. An attacker could manipulate these parameters to make the server send requests to arbitrary internal or external hosts.

### Impact

An attacker could force the Prebid Server to make requests to internal network resources, potentially extracting sensitive data from the host environment (e.g., cloud metadata, internal services) or bypassing firewall rules.

### Key diff

```
--- /dev/null
+++ b/util/urlutil/security.go
@@ -0,0 +1,12 @@
+package urlutil
+
+import "regexp"
+
+var safeHostPattern = regexp.MustCompile(`^[a-zA-Z0-9.-]+(:[0-9]+)?$`)
+
+// IsSafeHost returns true for bare hostnames with an optional port.
+// It intentionally rejects URL control characters such as '/', '?', '#', and '@'
+// so user-supplied host values cannot rewrite the outbound request URL.
+func IsSafeHost(host string) bool {
+	return safeHostPattern.MatchString(host)
+}

--- adapters/acuityads/acuityads.go
+++ b/adapters/acuityads/acuityads.go
@@ -107,6 +108,9 @@ func (a *AcuityAdsAdapter) buildEndpointURL(params *openrtb_ext.ExtAcuityAds) (string, error) {
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility function, `urlutil.IsSafeHost`, which validates user-supplied hostnames to ensure they do not contain URL control characters. This function is then applied to all user-controlled parameters that are used in constructing outbound request URLs, preventing attackers from injecting malicious URLs or paths.

## GHSA-mjqf-28ph-426h · github.com/kube-logging/logging-operator

2026-07-29 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The logging operator was vulnerable to Fluentd configuration injection because it did not properly validate or escape user-provided input before incorporating it into Fluentd configuration files. Specifically, newline characters in directive names, types, IDs, labels, log levels, tags, and parameter names, as well as parameter values, could break out of the intended configuration structure, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary Fluentd directives, including those that execute remote code.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary Fluentd configuration, leading to remote code execution on the Fluentd pods managed by the logging operator. This could compromise the entire Kubernetes cluster where the operator is deployed.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/sdk/logging/model/render/fluent.go
+++ b/pkg/sdk/logging/model/render/fluent.go
@@ -44,6 +44,19 @@ func (f *FluentRender) RenderDirectives(directives []types.Directive, indent int
 		if meta.Directive == "" {
 			return fmt.Errorf("directive must have a name %s", meta)
 		}
+		// Structural tokens can't be quoted, so a newline would break out.
+		for _, t := range []struct{ kind, value string }{
+			{"directive name", meta.Directive},
+			{"@type", meta.Type},
+			{"@id", meta.Id},
+			{"@label", meta.Label},
+			{"@log_level", meta.LogLevel},
+			{"tag", meta.Tag},
+		} {
+			if err := validateFluentToken(t.kind, t.value); err != nil {
+				return err
+			}
+		}
 		f.indentedf(indent, "<%s%s>", meta.Directive, tag(meta.Tag))
 		if meta.Type != "" {
 			f.indentedf(indent+f.Indent, "@type %s", meta.Type)
@@ -61,7 +74,10 @@ func (f *FluentRender) RenderDirectives(directives []types.Directive, indent int
 			keys := mapstrstr.Keys(params)
 			sort.Strings(keys)
 			for _, k := range keys {
-				f.indentedf(indent+f.Indent, "%s %s", k, params[k])
+				if err := validateFluentToken("parameter name", k); err != nil {
+					return err
+				}
+				f.indentedf(indent+f.Indent, "%s %s", k, escapeFluentValue(params[k]))
 			}
 		}
 		if sections := d.GetSections(); len(sections) > 0 {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces validation to prevent newline characters in Fluentd structural tokens (directive names, types, IDs, labels, log levels, tags, and parameter names). It also adds an `escapeFluentValue` function to properly quote and escape parameter values that contain newlines or '#' characters, preventing them from being interpreted as structural elements or Ruby interpolations.

## GHSA-38c3-wv3c-v3xj · swagger-typescript-api

2026-07-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.3 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `swagger-typescript-api` package was vulnerable to Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) because it would fetch external OpenAPI schemas and their `$ref` references without proper validation of the target URLs. An attacker could craft a malicious OpenAPI schema with `servers[0].url` or `$ref` fields pointing to internal network resources or sensitive services.

### Impact

An attacker could force the application to make requests to arbitrary internal network resources, potentially leading to information disclosure, port scanning, or interaction with internal services not intended to be exposed externally.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
+++ b/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
@@ -371,11 +389,19 @@ export class ResolvedSwaggerSchema {
     url: string,
   ): Promise<Maybe<AnyObject>> {
     try {
-      const response = await fetch(url, {
-        headers: this.getRemoteRequestHeaders(),
-      });
+      const response = await fetchRemoteSchemaResponse(
+        url,
+        {
+          headers: this.getRemoteRequestHeaders(url),
+        },
+        {
+          specSourceUrl:
+            typeof this.config.url === "string" ? this.config.url : undefined,
+          allowExplicitSpecUrl: this.isExplicitSpecUrl(url),
+        },
+      );
 
-      if (!response.ok) {
+      if (!response?.ok) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `remote-schema-fetch.ts` utility that implements strict validation for remote schema URLs. It checks if a target URL points to a public host and prevents fetching from private IP addresses, localhost, or blocked hostnames, unless the URL is explicitly the original spec source or from the same origin.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 110 occurrences**

## GHSA-5f94-x226-ccpm · swagger-typescript-api

2026-07-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.3 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `swagger-typescript-api` package could fetch external OpenAPI/Swagger schemas and their referenced external schemas without sufficient validation of the target URLs. This allowed an attacker to specify a URL that would resolve to an internal network resource or a local file, bypassing intended network restrictions.

### Impact

An attacker could perform Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) to access internal network resources, potentially leading to information disclosure, port scanning, or interaction with internal services that are not exposed to the public internet.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
+++ b/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
@@ -437,7 +463,13 @@ export class ResolvedSwaggerSchema {
         }
 
         const absoluteUrl = this.resolveAbsoluteUrl(externalPath, currentUrl);
-        if (absoluteUrl && !visited.has(absoluteUrl)) {
+        if (
+          absoluteUrl &&
+          !visited.has(absoluteUrl) &&
+          (await isRemoteSchemaFetchAllowed(absoluteUrl, {
+            specSourceUrl: this.config.url,
+          }))
+        ) {
           queue.push(absoluteUrl);
         }
       }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility module `remote-schema-fetch.ts` to validate remote schema URLs. It checks if a hostname resolves to a private IP address or is a blocked hostname (e.g., localhost) and restricts fetching to public hosts or the same origin as the initial spec URL. The `fetch` calls are updated to use this new validation logic.

## GHSA-hqj5-cw9f-rx67 · swagger-typescript-api

2026-07-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.3 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `swagger-typescript-api` package fetched external OpenAPI/Swagger schemas and their referenced external schemas without sufficient validation of the URLs. Specifically, it did not prevent fetching from private IP addresses or localhosts, allowing an attacker to craft a malicious `servers[0].url` or external schema reference to target internal network resources.

### Impact

An attacker could force the server-side application to make requests to arbitrary internal network resources, potentially leading to information disclosure, access to sensitive internal services, or interaction with other internal systems.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
+++ b/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
@@ -437,7 +463,13 @@ export class ResolvedSwaggerSchema {
         }
 
         const absoluteUrl = this.resolveAbsoluteUrl(externalPath, currentUrl);
-        if (absoluteUrl && !visited.has(absoluteUrl)) {
+        if (
+          absoluteUrl &&
+          !visited.has(absoluteUrl) &&
+          (await isRemoteSchemaFetchAllowed(absoluteUrl, {
+            specSourceUrl: this.config.url,
+          }))
+        ) {
           queue.push(absoluteUrl);
         }
       }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility file `remote-schema-fetch.ts` with functions to validate URLs. It checks if an IP address is private, if a hostname is blocked (e.g., localhost), and resolves hostnames to ensure all associated IP addresses are public. The `ResolvedSwaggerSchema` class now uses these checks before fetching any remote schema or external reference, preventing requests to private or blocked hosts.

## GHSA-w284-33mx-6g9v · swagger-typescript-api

2026-07-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.3 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `swagger-typescript-api` package was vulnerable to Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) because it fetched external OpenAPI schema references without sufficient validation of the target URLs. An attacker could supply a malicious OpenAPI specification containing references to internal network resources or sensitive cloud metadata endpoints.

### Impact

An attacker could force the server-side component generating the API to make requests to arbitrary internal or external URLs, potentially leading to information disclosure (e.g., cloud metadata, internal service banners) or interaction with internal services.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
+++ b/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
@@ -437,7 +463,13 @@
         }
 
         const absoluteUrl = this.resolveAbsoluteUrl(externalPath, currentUrl);
-        if (absoluteUrl && !visited.has(absoluteUrl)) {
+        if (
+          absoluteUrl &&
+          !visited.has(absoluteUrl) &&
+          (await isRemoteSchemaFetchAllowed(absoluteUrl, {
+            specSourceUrl: this.config.url,
+          }))
+        ) {
           queue.push(absoluteUrl);
         }
       }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces new utility functions (`remote-schema-fetch.ts`) to validate remote URLs before fetching. It checks if an IP address is private, resolves hostnames to ensure all associated IPs are public, and restricts fetching to the original spec's origin or explicitly public hosts. Authorization tokens are now only sent to the original spec's origin.

## GHSA-7h3g-4w2f-fj2f · proot-distro

2026-07-29 · Python · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The `proot-distro` tool allowed restoring a tar archive that could contain multiple container root filesystems. An attacker could craft a malicious archive to include entries for an unintended container, potentially overwriting its files or injecting malicious content, because the tool did not strictly enforce that an archive should only affect a single, explicitly targeted container.

### Impact

An attacker could overwrite files in an arbitrary container on the host system by crafting a malicious archive. This could lead to privilege escalation, arbitrary code execution within other containers, or disruption of other containerized environments.

### Key diff

```
--- a/proot_distro/commands/restore.py
+++ b/proot_distro/commands/restore.py
@@ -303,10 +315,14 @@ def _check_bare_root(member_name: str) -> bool:
                 if container_name is None:
                     continue
 
-                # Acquire exclusive lock for this container before
-                # touching it. Restore archives are streamed so we can't
-                # pre-scan the names; lock lazily on first sighting.
-                if container_name not in pending_locks:
+                # Only one container may be restored per archive. The first
+                # valid member fixes the target and acquires its exclusive
+                # lock; a member naming a different container is rejected so a
+                # multi-container archive can't overwrite more than the user
+                # asked for. Archives are streamed, so this is detected on the
+                # fly rather than by pre-scanning the member names.
+                if restore_name is None:
+                    restore_name = container_name
                     lock = ContainerLock(
                         container_name, exclusive=True, command="restore"
                     )
@@ -316,12 +332,18 @@ def _check_bare_root(member_name: str) -> bool:
                         log_error(f"Cannot restore: container "
                                   f"'{container_name}' is busy{hint}.")
                         sys.exit(1)
-                    pending_locks[container_name] = lock
+                elif container_name != restore_name:
+                    clear_bar()
+                    log_error(f"Cannot restore: archive contains more than "
+                              f"one container ('{restore_name}' and "
+                              f"'{container_name}'). Restore handles a single "
+                              f"container at a time.")
+                    sys.exit(1)
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the restore logic to strictly enforce that an archive can only restore a single container. It now identifies the target container from the first valid member in the archive and rejects any subsequent members that refer to a different container, preventing multi-container archives from silently overwriting unintended targets.

## GHSA-qcxp-gm7m-4j5v · io.quarkus:quarkus-vertx-http

2026-07-29 · Java · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an inconsistency in path normalization between the security layer and downstream handlers. The security policies (authentication and authorization) were applied to a partially normalized path, while the actual request handlers might process a more fully normalized path. This discrepancy allowed attackers to craft specially malformed URLs that would bypass security checks but still reach the intended resource.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authentication and authorization checks, gaining unauthorized access to protected resources or functionalities within the Quarkus application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/extensions/vertx-http/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/vertx/http/runtime/security/AbstractPathMatchingHttpSecurityPolicy.java
+++ b/extensions/vertx-http/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/vertx/http/runtime/security/AbstractPathMatchingHttpSecurityPolicy.java
@@ -194,7 +194,7 @@ private static void addPermissionToPathMatcher(Map<String, HttpSecurityPolicy> p
             HttpMatcher m = new HttpMatcher(httpPermission.getAuthMechanisms(), httpPermission.getMethods(), policy);
             List<HttpMatcher> perms = new ArrayList<>();
             perms.add(m);
-            builder.addPath(path, perms);
+            builder.addPath(HttpSecurityUtils.normalizePath(path), perms);
         }
     }
 
@@ -210,7 +210,7 @@ private static List<HttpSecurityPolicy> findPermissionCheckers(RoutingContext co
     private static List<HttpMatcher> findHttpMatchers(RoutingContext context,
             ImmutablePathMatcher<List<HttpMatcher>> pathMatcher) {
         String normalizedPath = context.normalizedPath();
-        PathMatch<List<HttpMatcher>> toCheck = pathMatcher.match(HttpSecurityUtils.pathWithoutMatrixParams(normalizedPath));
+        PathMatch<List<HttpMatcher>> toCheck = pathMatcher.match(HttpSecurityUtils.normalizePath(normalizedPath));
         if (toCheck.getValue() == null || toCheck.getValue().isEmpty()) {
             return List.of();
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `HttpSecurityUtils.normalizePath` method that performs comprehensive path normalization, including full percent-decoding, stripping matrix parameters, removing null bytes, normalizing backslashes, and resolving dot segments. This method is now consistently applied when defining security policies and when matching incoming requests against these policies, ensuring that the security layer and application handlers operate on the same canonical path.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 55 occurrences**

## GHSA-h754-fxp7-88wx · swagger-typescript-api

2026-07-29 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application fetched remote OpenAPI specifications and their referenced external schemas without sufficient validation of the target URLs. This allowed an attacker to craft a malicious spec that would cause the application to make requests to arbitrary internal or external resources, potentially exfiltrating sensitive authorization tokens.

### Impact

An attacker could exfiltrate authorization tokens by redirecting requests to an attacker-controlled server or access internal network resources if the application was running in an environment with access to such resources.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
+++ b/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
@@ -371,11 +389,19 @@ export class ResolvedSwaggerSchema {
     url: string,
   ): Promise<Maybe<AnyObject>> {
     try {
-      const response = await fetch(url, {
-        headers: this.getRemoteRequestHeaders(),
-      });
+      const response = await fetchRemoteSchemaResponse(
+        url,
+        {
+          headers: this.getRemoteRequestHeaders(url),
+        },
+        {
+          specSourceUrl:
+            typeof this.config.url === "string" ? this.config.url : undefined,
+          allowExplicitSpecUrl: this.isExplicitSpecUrl(url),
+        },
+      );
 
-      if (!response.ok) {
+      if (!response?.ok) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility module for remote schema fetching with robust validation. It checks if a target URL is a public host, prevents fetching from private IP addresses or blocked hostnames (e.g., localhost), and ensures authorization tokens are only sent to the original spec's HTTP origin or explicitly allowed URLs.

## GHSA-cg9x-g3gm-h5h6 · org.verapdf:validation-model-jakarta

2026-07-29 · Java · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `XML_EXTERNAL_ENTITY→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application used the default configuration for `DocumentBuilderFactory` when parsing XML content from untrusted PDF files. By default, `DocumentBuilderFactory` does not disable external entity processing, allowing an attacker to include external entities in the XML.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PDF file containing XML with external entities, leading to the disclosure of local files on the server, or potentially other system-level attacks depending on the XML parser's capabilities and network access.

### Key diff

```
--- DocumentBuilder builder = DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance().newDocumentBuilder();
+++ DocumentBuilder builder = SecureXML.newSafeDocumentBuilder();
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the direct instantiation of `DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance().newDocumentBuilder()` with a call to `SecureXML.newSafeDocumentBuilder()`. This new method configures the `DocumentBuilder` to explicitly disable external entity processing, preventing XXE attacks.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 6 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, C#, Java, Ruby
> → GHSA-3crg-w4f6-42mx
> → GHSA-fwvm-ggf6-2p4x
> → GHSA-7v6w-c3f4-9wpq
> → GHSA-36mm-w85j-3q2j
> → GHSA-xr9x-r78c-5hrm

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/veraPDF/veraPDF-validation/commit/94caa46c1a594512247fbd46c808edae39469542)

## GHSA-x36r-4347-pm5x · swagger-typescript-api

2026-07-29 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.1 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `swagger-typescript-api` library could fetch external OpenAPI/Swagger specifications referenced via `$ref` without proper validation of the target URL. This allowed an attacker to supply a malicious URL that would cause the server to make requests to internal network resources or other sensitive locations.

### Impact

An attacker could force the server to make requests to arbitrary URLs, potentially accessing internal services, cloud metadata endpoints, or other sensitive resources that are not directly exposed to the internet.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
+++ b/src/resolved-swagger-schema.ts
@@ -437,7 +463,13 @@ export class ResolvedSwaggerSchema {
         }
 
         const absoluteUrl = this.resolveAbsoluteUrl(externalPath, currentUrl);
-        if (absoluteUrl && !visited.has(absoluteUrl)) {
+        if (
+          absoluteUrl &&
+          !visited.has(absoluteUrl) &&
+          (await isRemoteSchemaFetchAllowed(absoluteUrl, {
+            specSourceUrl: this.config.url,
+          }))
+        ) {
           queue.push(absoluteUrl);
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `remote-schema-fetch.ts` utility that implements strict validation for remote schema fetches. It checks if the target URL is a public host and prevents requests to private IP addresses (IPv4 and IPv6) and blocked hostnames like `localhost`. It also ensures that authorization tokens are only sent to the original spec source URL.

## GHSA-pc2w-4mq8-32qw · @dynatrace-oss/dynatrace-mcp-server

2026-07-29 · JavaScript · **LOW** 3.7 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application allowed the creation of Dynatrace notebooks without requiring explicit human approval. This meant that any authenticated user could create notebooks, potentially leading to unauthorized resource creation or configuration changes.

### Impact

An attacker could create Dynatrace notebooks without proper oversight, potentially leading to unauthorized resource consumption, configuration changes, or the execution of arbitrary DQL queries within the Dynatrace environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/index.ts
+++ b/src/index.ts
@@ -1594,8 +1594,17 @@ You can now execute new Grail queries (DQL, etc.) again. If this happens more of
       },
       async ({ name, content, description }) => {
+        const approved = await requestHumanApproval(`Create Dynatrace notebook: "${name}"`);
+
+        if (!approved) {
+          return 'Operation cancelled: Human approval was not granted for creating this notebook.';
+        }
+
         const dtClient = await createAuthenticatedHttpClient(allRequiredScopes);
         const data = await createDynatraceNotebook(dtClient, name, content, description);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a human approval gate before a Dynatrace notebook can be created. It calls `requestHumanApproval` and, if approval is not granted, the operation is cancelled, preventing unauthorized notebook creation.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 93 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, Ruby, JavaScript, Python, PHP, Java, RUST, Erlang, C#

## GHSA-8hm4-r66f-29wr · alextselegidis/easyappointments

2026-07-29 · PHP · **LOW** 3.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application stored the 'oauth_provider_id' in the session without proper validation or authorization checks. Any authenticated backend user could manipulate this session variable to rebind the Google sync settings of another provider, as the system only checked if an ID existed, not if the current user was authorized to modify that specific provider's settings.

### Impact

An attacker, as a backend user, could rebind the Google Calendar synchronization of any other provider account to their own Google account, potentially disrupting their scheduling or gaining unauthorized access to their calendar data.

### Key diff

```
-        $oauth_provider_id = session('oauth_provider_id');
+        $oauth_provider_id = filter_var(session('oauth_provider_id'), FILTER_VALIDATE_INT);
+        $user_id = (int) session('user_id');
+
+        if ($oauth_provider_id && $oauth_provider_id > 0) {
+            if (cannot('edit', PRIV_USERS) && $user_id !== (int) $oauth_provider_id) {
+                show_error('Forbidden', 403);
+
+                return;
+            }
 
-        if ($oauth_provider_id) {
             $this->providers_model->set_setting($oauth_provider_id, 'google_sync', true);
             $this->providers_model->set_setting($oauth_provider_id, 'google_token', json_encode($token));
             $this->providers_model->set_setting($oauth_provider_id, 'google_calendar', 'primary');
+            session(['oauth_provider_id' => null]);
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds validation to the 'oauth_provider_id' by casting it to an integer and performing an authorization check. It ensures that if the current user does not have 'edit' privileges for users, they can only modify their own provider's Google sync settings, preventing unauthorized rebindings. The 'oauth_provider_id' is also nulled out from the session after use.

## GHSA-36mm-w85j-3q2j · org.verapdf:validation-model-jakarta

2026-07-29 · Java · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `XML_EXTERNAL_ENTITY→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application parsed XML input from PDF documents using DocumentBuilderFactory without disabling external entity processing. This allowed an attacker to supply a malicious XML document containing external entity declarations, which the parser would then resolve.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary files from the server's filesystem, potentially including sensitive configuration files or user data. In some environments, it could also lead to server-side request forgery (SSRF) or remote code execution (RCE) if specific wrappers are enabled.

### Key diff

```
--- a/validation-model/src/main/java/org/verapdf/gf/model/impl/pd/GFPDAcroForm.java
+++ b/validation-model/src/main/java/org/verapdf/gf/model/impl/pd/GFPDAcroForm.java
@@ -104,8 +104,7 @@ public String getdynamicRender() {
         } else {
             return null;
         }
-            DocumentBuilder builder = DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance().newDocumentBuilder();
-            builder.setErrorHandler(null);
+            DocumentBuilder builder = SecureXML.newSafeDocumentBuilder();
             Document doc = builder.parse(new InputSource(inputStream));
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the direct instantiation of DocumentBuilderFactory with a call to SecureXML.newSafeDocumentBuilder(). This utility method configures the DocumentBuilder to explicitly disable external entity processing, preventing XXE attacks.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 6 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, C#, Java, Ruby
> → GHSA-3crg-w4f6-42mx
> → GHSA-fwvm-ggf6-2p4x
> → GHSA-7v6w-c3f4-9wpq
> → GHSA-cg9x-g3gm-h5h6
> → GHSA-xr9x-r78c-5hrm

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/veraPDF/veraPDF-validation/commit/94caa46c1a594512247fbd46c808edae39469542)

## GHSA-7c26-995w-6f47 · org.verapdf:parser

2026-07-29 · Java · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The veraPDF parser did not properly validate the size of arrays or the number of iterations in 'for' loops within PostScript Type 1 font programs. Additionally, it lacked a recursion depth limit for the 'toExecute' method. An attacker could craft a malicious Type 1 font program with excessively large array sizes, long-running loops, or deep recursion, leading to resource exhaustion or an infinite loop.

### Impact

An attacker could cause the veraPDF parser to consume excessive CPU and memory resources, leading to a denial of service for applications processing the malicious PDF documents. This could make the application unresponsive or crash it.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/main/java/org/verapdf/parser/postscript/PSOperator.java
+++ b/src/main/java/org/verapdf/parser/postscript/PSOperator.java
@@ -35,6 +35,8 @@
  * @author Sergey Shemyakov
  */
 public class PSOperator extends PSObject {
+    private static final int MAX_PS_ARRAY_SIZE = 1 << 16;
+    private static final int MAX_PS_FOR_ITERATIONS = 10_000;
     private Stack<COSObject> operandStack;
     private Map<ASAtom, COSObject> userDict;
     private final String operator;
@@ -536,6 +538,9 @@ private void load() throws PostScriptException {
     private void array() throws PostScriptException {
         try {
             int arraySize = getTopNumber().getInteger().intValue();
+            if (arraySize < 0 || arraySize > MAX_PS_ARRAY_SIZE) {
+                throw new PostScriptException("Array size " + arraySize + " is out of range");
+            }
             COSObject array = COSArray.construct(arraySize);
             for (int i = 0; i < arraySize; i++) {
                 array.add(COSObject.getEmpty());
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces limits for PostScript array sizes (MAX_PS_ARRAY_SIZE), 'for' loop iterations (MAX_PS_FOR_ITERATIONS), and recursion depth for the 'toExecute' method (MAX_TO_EXECUTE_DEPTH). It also adds checks to ensure these limits are not exceeded and throws a PostScriptException if they are, preventing resource exhaustion.

## GHSA-jq8w-8q2f-ffm9 · github.com/zitadel/zitadel

2026-07-29 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The ZITADEL API allowed users to self-verify their email and phone numbers, and to request verification codes, without proper authorization checks. The `checkPermissionUpdateUser` function was called with `allowSelfManagement` set to `true` unconditionally, effectively bypassing the necessary write permissions for these sensitive operations.

### Impact

An attacker could self-verify their email or phone number, or request verification codes for their account, even if they should not have the necessary permissions. This could lead to account takeover or bypass of multi-factor authentication if combined with other vulnerabilities.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/command/user_v2_email.go
+++ b/internal/command/user_v2_email.go
@@ -148,7 +148,8 @@ func (c *Commands) changeUserEmailWithGeneratorEvents(ctx context.Context, userI
 	if err != nil {
 		return nil, err
 	}
-	if err = c.checkPermissionUpdateUser(ctx, cmd.aggregate.ResourceOwner, userID, true); err != nil {
+	allowSelfManagement := !returnCode
+	if err = c.checkPermissionUpdateUser(ctx, cmd.aggregate.ResourceOwner, userID, allowSelfManagement); err != nil {
 		return nil, err
 	}
 	if err = cmd.Change(ctx, domain.EmailAddress(email)); err != nil
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `checkPermissionUpdateUser` calls to conditionally set `allowSelfManagement` based on whether a verification code is being returned (`!returnCode`) or if the email/phone is being marked as verified or a code is being returned (`human.Email.Verified || human.Email.ReturnCode`). This ensures that self-management is only allowed for operations that do not involve self-verification or code generation without proper permissions.

## GHSA-jrmc-qg6p-94fp · org.verapdf:parser

2026-07-29 · Java · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The veraPDF parser did not properly validate the size of arrays or the number of iterations in 'for' loops within PostScript CMap streams. An attacker could craft a malicious PostScript stream with excessively large array sizes or 'for' loops, leading to resource exhaustion or infinite loops.

### Impact

An attacker could cause the veraPDF parser to consume excessive memory or CPU resources, leading to a denial of service for applications processing malicious PDF documents.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/main/java/org/verapdf/parser/postscript/PSOperator.java
+++ b/src/main/java/org/verapdf/parser/postscript/PSOperator.java
@@ -536,6 +538,9 @@ private void load() throws PostScriptException {

+            if (arraySize < 0 || arraySize > MAX_PS_ARRAY_SIZE) {
+                throw new PostScriptException("Array size " + arraySize + " is out of range");
+            }
             COSObject array = COSArray.construct(arraySize);
             for (int i = 0; i < arraySize; i++) {
                 array.add(COSObject.getEmpty());
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces limits for PostScript array sizes (MAX_PS_ARRAY_SIZE), 'for' loop iterations (MAX_PS_FOR_ITERATIONS), and recursion depth for 'toExecute' calls (MAX_TO_EXECUTE_DEPTH). It also adds checks to ensure array sizes are non-negative and within the defined limits, and that 'for' loop increments are valid to prevent infinite loops.

## GHSA-px9f-whj3-246m · req

2026-07-29 · Erlang · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

The application was vulnerable to header injection because it did not properly escape user-controlled input (filename, content_type, and name) when constructing multipart form-data headers. This allowed attackers to inject newlines and arbitrary header fields.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary headers into the multipart form-data, potentially leading to various issues depending on how the receiving server processes these headers, such as cache poisoning or unexpected application behavior.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/req/utils.ex
+++ b/lib/req/utils.ex
@@ -574,18 +574,19 @@ defmodule Req.Utils do
 
     params =
       if filename = options[:filename] do
-        ["; filename=\"", filename, "\""]
+        ["; filename=\"", escape_form_param(filename), "\""]
       else
         []
       end
 
     headers =
       if content_type = options[:content_type] do
-        ["content-type: ", content_type, @crlf]
+        ["content-type: ", escape_form_param(content_type), @crlf]
       else
         []
       end
 
+    name = escape_form_param(Atom.to_string(name))
     headers = ["content-disposition: form-data; name=\"#{name}"", params, @crlf, headers]
     header = [["--", boundary, @crlf, headers, @crlf]]
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an `escape_form_param` function that uses `URI.encode` to escape double quotes, carriage returns, and line feeds from user-supplied values for filename, content_type, and the form part name. This prevents the injection of newlines and other special characters into the multipart headers.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 14 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, PHP, Python, Erlang, Go, Java
> → GHSA-fvcv-3m26-pcqx
> → GHSA-247c-9743-5963
> → GHSA-c3h8-g69v-pjrg
> → GHSA-gv7r-3mr9-h5x8

## GHSA-xgr6-pqjv-3pf8 · alextselegidis/easyappointments

2026-07-29 · PHP · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The application was disclosing sensitive customer Personally Identifiable Information (PII) on the booking reschedule page without proper authorization or filtering. When a customer object was retrieved, all its fields were made available, including potentially sensitive ones, to an unauthenticated user who could access the reschedule page.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could access and view sensitive customer PII, such as names, email addresses, phone numbers, or other personal details, by simply navigating to a specific booking reschedule URL.

### Key diff

```
--- a/application/controllers/Booking.php
+++ b/application/controllers/Booking.php
@@ -280,6 +280,7 @@ public function index(): void
                 return;
             }
             $customer = $this->customers_model->find($appointment['id_users_customer']);
+            $this->customers_model->only($customer, $this->allowed_customer_fields);
             $customer_token = md5(uniqid(mt_rand(), true));
 
             // Cache the token for 10 minutes.
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a filtering mechanism to restrict the fields of the customer object that are exposed. By calling `$this->customers_model->only($customer, $this->allowed_customer_fields);`, only explicitly allowed fields are retained, preventing the disclosure of sensitive PII.

## GHSA-xvg2-cgv6-6h7v · github.com/tinfoil-factory/netfoil

2026-07-29 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `generateBlockResponse` function was designed to create a DNS response that explicitly returned null IP addresses (0.0.0.0 or ::) for blocked domains. However, some DNS resolvers, particularly those on localhost, might interpret these null responses as valid and resolve them to localhost, effectively bypassing the intended blocking mechanism.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially bypass DNS blocking policies, allowing traffic to reach internal or otherwise restricted localhost services that should have been inaccessible.

### Key diff

```
--- a/dns/filter.go
+++ b/dns/filter.go
@@ -638,54 +638,15 @@ func (p *Policy) ipv6IsAllowed(ipString string) (bool, FilterReason) {
 	return false, FilterReason(reason)
 }
 
-func generateBlockResponse(question Question) *Response {
-	// TODO make response type configurable? null, NXDOMAIN and NODATA
-	domain := question.Name
-	recordType := question.Type
-
+func generateBlockResponse() *Response {
 	var response *Response
 	flags := Flags{
-		RCODE: ResponseCodeNoError,
+		RCODE: ResponseCodeNXDomain,
 	}
 
-	if recordType == RecordTypeA || recordType == RecordTypeAAAA {
-		response = &Response{
-			Flags: flags,
-			Answers: []Answer{
-				{
-					Name:  domain,
-					Type:  recordType,
-					Class: ClassTypeIN,
-					TTL:   defaultTTL,
-					IPv4:  ipv4Null,
-					IPv6:  ipv6Null,
-				},
-			},
-		}
-	} else if recordType == RecordTypeHTTPS {
-		record := HTTPSRecord{
-			Priority:   1,
-			TargetName: ".",
-			ALPN:       []string{},
-			IPv4Hint:   []net.IP{ipv4Null},
-			IPv6Hint:   []net.IP{ipv6Null},
-		}
-
-		response = &Response{
-			Flags: flags,
-			Answers: []Answer{
-				{
-					Name:        domain,
-					Type:        recordType,
-					Class:       ClassTypeIN,
-					TTL:         defaultTTL,
-					HTTPSRecord: record,
-				},
-			},
-		}
-	} else {
-		// FIXME return null response rather than NODATA
-		response = generateNoDataResponse()
+	response = &Response{
+		Flags:   flags,
+		Answers: nil,
 	}
 
 	return response
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `generateBlockResponse` function to return an `NXDOMAIN` (Non-Existent Domain) response code with no answers, instead of a response containing null IP addresses. This ensures that blocked domains are unequivocally reported as non-existent, preventing misinterpretation by resolvers.
